.
Hence there is a surjective morphism
\mu\colon\pi_{1}((W^{\prime\prime}\cap\widehat{X}^{\circ})_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sm}}})\rightarrow G_{Y}\times\mathbb{Z}^{k^{\prime\prime}}
, such that
\sigma_{i}=\chi_{i}\circ\mu
for
i=1,2
, where

\chi_{1}\colon G_{Y}\times\mathbb{Z}^{k^{\prime\prime}}\rightarrow\pi_{1}(Y_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sm}}}\times\check{S}^{\circ})

and

\chi_{2}\colon G_{Y}\times\mathbb{Z}^{k^{\prime\prime}}\rightarrow\pi_{1}(Y_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sm}}}\times(\mathbb{C}^{n}\setminus H^{\prime}))

are injective morphisms.
Furthermore, the cokernel of
\chi_{1}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}^{k-k^{\prime\prime}}
and the one of
\chi_{2}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}^{k^{\prime}-k^{\prime\prime}}
, where
k^{\prime}
is the number of irreducible components of
H^{\prime}
.

We notice that, if
{\nu^{-1}(W^{\prime\prime})}_{\circ}
is a connected component of
{\nu^{-1}(W^{\prime\prime})}
,
then
\nu|_{\nu^{-1}(W^{\prime\prime})_{\circ}}\colon{\nu^{-1}(W^{\prime\prime})}_{\circ}\rightarrow W^{\prime\prime}
is induced by the kernel of the composition of the following sequence of morphisms of groups

\textstyle{\pi_{1}((W^{\prime\prime}\cap\widehat{X}^{\circ})_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sm}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{G_{Y}\times\mathbb{Z}^{k^{\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\chi_{1}}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(Y_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sm}}}\times\check{S}^{\circ})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(Y_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sm}}}\times\check{S}^{\circ})/\mathcal{N},}

which is equal to
\mu^{-1}(\chi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}))
.
Since the cokernel of
\chi_{1}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}^{k-k^{\prime}}
,
the subgroup
\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}:=\chi_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{N})
of
G_{Y}\times\mathbb{Z}^{k^{\prime\prime}}
is the canonical subgroup defined in Lemma
6.5
.
Then
\nu|_{\nu^{-1}(W^{\prime\prime})_{\circ}}
is induced by the subgroup
\mu^{-1}(\mathcal{N^{\prime\prime}})
.
Similarly, if
{(\nu^{\prime})^{-1}(W^{\prime\prime})}_{\circ}
is a connected component of
{(\nu^{\prime})^{-1}(W^{\prime\prime})}
,
then the finite morphism
\nu^{\prime}|_{(\nu^{\prime})^{-1}(W^{\prime\prime})_{\circ}}\colon{(\nu^{\prime})^{-1}(W^{\prime\prime})}_{\circ}\rightarrow W^{\prime\prime}
is induced by the subgroup
\mu^{-1}(\chi_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{N^{\prime}}))
.
The same argument implies that
\chi_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{N^{\prime}})
is the canonical subgroup of
G_{Y}\times\mathbb{Z}^{k^{\prime\prime}}
in Lemma
6.5
.
Hence
\chi_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{N})=\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}
, and we deduce that
\nu|_{\nu^{-1}(W^{\prime\prime})_{\circ}}
is isomorphic to
\nu^{\prime}|_{(\nu^{\prime})^{-1}(W^{\prime\prime})_{\circ}}
.
This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

To end this section, we observe that the item (2) of Setup
6.6
on the divisibility of vanishing orders can be achieved by taking cyclic covers.

Lemma 6.13
.

With the notation in the item (1) of Setup
6.6
, we let

\overline{S}=\check{S}[\sqrt[6]{T_{1}},...,\sqrt[6]{T_{n}}]
and let

\overline{M}=M\times_{\check{S}}\overline{S}.

We denote by
\overline{H}_{i}
the preimage of
H_{i}
in
\overline{S}
.
Then
(\overline{S}_{i},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{H_{i}})
is snc and

M
is a hypersurface in
\mathbb{D}^{3}\times\overline{S}
defined by a function of the shape of Proposition
5.2
with respect to
(\overline{S}_{i},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{H_{i}})
, such that the vanishing orders of the functions
\alpha
and
\beta
on
\overline{S}
along each
\overline{H}_{i}
are divisible by 6.

Proof.

In order to fix the notation, we assume without loss of the generality that the defining function of
M
is

F(x,y,z,s)=x^{2}+\alpha(s)y^{3}+\beta(s)\cdot y(\gamma(s)z+u(y,s)y)^{3}.

By Lemma
2.7
, the pair
(\overline{S},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{H_{i}})
is snc.
Indeed,
\overline{S}
is also isomorphic to a neighborhood of the origin in
\mathbb{C}^{n}
,
and
\overline{H}_{i}
is isomorphic to a coordinate hyperplane in
\overline{S}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{n}
.
Then
\overline{M}
is the hypersurface in
\mathbb{D}^{3}\times\overline{S}
define by the function

\overline{F}(x,y,z,\overline{s})=x^{2}+\alpha(p(\overline{s}))y^{3}+\beta(p(\overline{s}))\cdot y(\gamma(p(\overline{s}))z+u(y,p(\overline{s}))y)^{3},

where
\overline{s}\in\overline{S}
is a point and
p\colon\overline{S}\rightarrow\check{S}
is the natural projection.
From the construction, we deduce directly that the vanishing orders of
\alpha\circ p
and
\beta\circ p
along each
\overline{H}_{i}
are divisible by 6.
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

7.
Modification around an irreducible singular locus

The objective of this section is to prove the following proposition, it combines the steps from Section
4
to Section
6
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
X
be a complex analytic variety with klt singularities.
Assume that
S\subseteq X_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sing}}}
is a compact irreducible component of codimension 2 in
X
.
Then there is a projective bimeromorphic morphism
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
, with
Y
normal, such that the following properties hold.
Let
\Delta
be the whole reduced exceptional divisor of
f
.

(1)

There is a
f
-ample and
f
-exceptional divisor.

(2)

The indeterminacy locus of
f^{-1}
is a proper closed subset of
S
.
In particular, we can define the strict transform
S_{Y}
of
S
in
Y
.

(3)

There is a normal complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{W}=(W_{i},G_{i},\psi_{i})_{i\in I}
, such that the quotient space
W
is an open neighborhood of
S_{Y}
in
Y
.

(4)

The divisorial critical locus of
\psi_{i}\colon W_{i}\rightarrow W
is contained in
\Delta
.

(5)

W_{i}
is smooth on
\psi_{i}^{-1}(W\setminus\Delta)
.

We first fix the following setup for the section.

Setup 7.2
.

We consider the data
X
,
\mathfrak{X}
,
S
and
H
as follows.
Let
X
be a complex analytic variety.
Assume that
S\subseteq X_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sing}}}
is a compact irreducible component of codimension 2.
We are only interested in the behavior of some neighborhood of
S
in
X
.
Let
H\subseteq S
be a proper closed subset,
and let
\mathfrak{X}=(X_{i},G_{i},\pi_{i})_{i\in I}
be a complex analytic orbispace,
with a finite family
I
of orbifold charts,
whose quotient space is
X
.
We set
S_{i}=\pi_{i}^{-1}(S)
and
H_{i}=\pi_{i}^{-1}(H)
.
Assume that
S_{i}
is an irreducible component of the singular locus of
X_{i}
,
and that
X_{i}
has the same type of canonical singularities at points of
S_{i}\setminus H_{i}
.
Furthermore, the intersection of the divisorial critical locus of
X_{i}\rightarrow X
and
S
is contained in
H
.

We will prove Proposition
7.1
in several steps, and the sketch is as follows.
Since
X
has klt singularities, there is a complex analytic orbispace
(X_{i},G_{i})
induced by the local index-one covers of
\omega_{X}
, see Example
2.12
.
The difficult case is when
X_{i}
is singular around the preimage of
S
.
To study this case, we may assume the situation of Setup
7.2
.
We first apply Proposition
4.1
on each orbifold chart
X_{i}
.
Then we can obtain some projective bimeromorphic morphism
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
.
We then locate on a neighborhood
U
of the strict transform
S_{Y}
of
S
in
Y
.
Replacing
X
by
U
and
S
by
S_{Y}
, we can assume that each
X_{i}
satisfies
the conclusion of Proposition
4.1
.
As a result, we are able to apply Proposition
5.2
on each
X_{i}
.
Afterward, we may assume that each
X_{i}
satisfies the conclusion of Proposition
5.2
.
Now we can apply Proposition
6.7
on each
X_{i}
.
This will complete the proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Lemma 7.3
.

With the notation of Setup
7.2
, there is a projective bimeromorphic morphism
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
such that the following properties hold.

(1)

There is a
f
-exceptional and
f
-ample divisor.

(2)

The indeterminacy locus of
f^{-1}
is a proper closed subset of
S
.
In particular, we can define the strict transform
S_{Y}
of
S
in
Y

(3)

The union of the intersection of
S_{Y}
with the
f
-exceptional locus and the preimage
(f|_{S_{Y}})^{-1}(H)
is pure of codimension 1 in
S_{Y}
.
We denote it by
H_{Y}
.

(4)

There is a complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{Y}=(Y_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
whose quotient space is
Y
.

(5)

Let
(S_{Y})_{i}
and
(H_{Y})_{i}
be the preimage of
S_{Y}
and
H_{Y}
respectively in
Y_{i}
.
Then
(S_{Y})_{i}
is smooth and
H_{Y}
induces a snc orbi-divisor on the orbifold
((S_{Y})_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
.

(6)

For every point
o\in(S_{Y})_{i}
, there is an open neighborhood
U
of
o
in
Y_{i}
, such that
U
is isomorphic to the hypersurface in
\mathbb{D}^{3}\times((S_{Y})_{i}\cap U)
defined by a function of standard form with respect to
((S_{Y})_{i}\cap U,(H_{Y})_{i}\cap U)
, see Section
4
.

(7)

Y_{i}
has the same type of canonical singularities at points of
(S_{Y})_{i}\setminus(H_{Y})_{i}
.

Proof.

For each
i
, we let
f_{i}\colon Y_{i}\rightarrow X_{i}
be the construction of Proposition
4.1
, with respect to
H_{i}\subseteq S_{i}\subseteq X_{i}
.
Since the construction of Proposition
4.1
is functorial, we see that there is a natural action of
G_{i}
on
Y_{i}
, and the family
(Y_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
defines a complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{Y}
.
Let
Y
be its quotient space.
Then there is an induced bimeromorphic morphism
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
.
Since each step in the construction of
f_{i}
is the blowup of some ideal sheaf, which is compatible along the overlaps,
there is a
f_{i}
-exceptional and
f_{i}
-ample divisor
E_{i}
which is compatible along the overlaps.
The collection
(E_{i})_{i\in I}
induces a
f
-exceptional and
f
-ample divisor in
Y
.
This proves the items (1), (2) and (4).

We note that
(S_{Y})_{i}
is the strict transform of
S_{i}
in
Y_{i}
, hence is smooth.
We also note that
(H_{Y})_{i}
is the union of the preimage of
H_{i}
in
(S_{Y})_{i}
and the intersection of
(S_{Y})_{i}
with the
f_{i}
-exceptional locus.
By construction, every
(H_{Y})_{i}
is a snc divisor in
(S_{Y})_{i}
, and
Y_{i}
has the same type of canonical singularities around
(S_{Y})_{i}\setminus(H_{Y})_{i}
.
This proves the item (3) and (7).
The item (6) also follows from the construction of Proposition
4.1
.

It remains to prove the item (5).
To this end, we can apply Lemma
2.16
to the orbi-divisor
((H_{Y})_{i})_{i\in I}
in the complex orbifold
((S_{Y})_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
.
Since
(H_{Y})_{i}
is a snc divisor in
(S_{Y})_{i}
,
the construction of Lemma
2.16
is to successively blowing up certain strata of
(H_{Y})_{i}
, which are compatible along the overlaps.
Hence we can blow up
Y_{i}
at the same centers, see Subsection
3.B
.
These blowups are again compatible along the overlaps.
Let
Y^{\prime}_{i}\rightarrow Y_{i}
be this blowup.
Then we obtain a complex analytic orbi-space
(Y_{i}^{\prime},G_{i})_{i\in I}
with quotient space
Y^{\prime}
.
Replacing
Y
by
Y^{\prime}
, we can assume that
H_{Y}\subseteq S_{Y}
induces a snc orbi-divisor on the orbifold
((S_{Y})_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
.
This proves the item (5) and completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We recall that the constructions in Section
5
and Section
6
are local around each point of the singular locus.
In the following lemma, we replace the family
(X_{i},G_{i},\pi_{i})_{i\in I}
of orbifold charts by a refinement, so that we can apply a local construction on each
X_{i}
entirely.

Lemma 7.4
.

With the notation of Setup
7.2
,
we assume that
\mathfrak{X}=(X_{i},G_{i},\pi_{i})_{i\in I}
,
S
and
H
satisfies the properties of

\mathfrak{Y}=(Y_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
,
S_{Y}
and
H_{Y}
in Lemma
7.3
.
Then, up to shrinking
X
around
S
, and up to replacing
(X_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
by an equivalent finite family of orbifold charts,
we can assume in addition the following properties.

(1)

If
n=\operatorname{dim}S
, then
S_{i}
is isomorphic to a contractible neighborhood of the origin in
\mathbb{C}^{n}
.
Moreover,
H_{i}
is isomorphic to the union of certain coordinate hyperplanes, and the origin is fixed by the action of
G_{i}
.

(2)

X_{i}
is isomorphic to an neighborhood of
\{\mathbf{0}_{3}\}\times S_{i}
in the hypersurface in
\mathbb{D}^{3}\times S_{i}
defined by an equation of standard form with respect to
(S_{i},H_{i})
.
In addition, the composite inclusion
S_{i}\subseteq X_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{D}^{3}\times S_{i}
is identified with
\{\mathbf{0}_{3}\}\times S_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{D}^{3}\times S_{i}
.

(3)

There is an integer
N>0
, such that Proposition
5.2
holds for each
H_{i}\subseteq S_{i}\subseteq X_{i}
with
N
, without shrinking
S_{i}
.

Proof.

From the item (6) of Lemma
7.3
,
for each point
x\in S_{i}\subseteq X_{i}
,
we can take a neighborhood
U_{x}\subseteq X_{i}
of it,
so that the property (2) of the present lemma holds on
U_{x}
.
Furthermore, we can assume that Proposition
5.2
holds on
U_{x}
entirely,
with respect to
H_{i}\cap U_{x}\subseteq S_{i}\cap U_{x}\subseteq U_{x}
.
Let
G_{x}
be the subgroup of
G_{i}
fixing
x
.
By shrinking
U_{x}
, we can assume that
(U_{x},G_{x})
is an orbifold chart
compatible with
\mathfrak{X}
.
Since
S_{i}
is smooth, the action of
G_{x}
on the germ
(x\in S_{i})
is isomorphic to a linear action (see
[
Car54
, Lemme 1]
or
[
Kaw88
, Lemma 9.9]
).
Thus, up to shrinking
U_{x}
, we can assume that
S_{i}\cap U_{x}
is isomorphic to a small enough contractible neighborhood of the origin in
\mathbb{C}^{n}
.
Furthermore,
H_{i}\cap U_{x}
is isomorphic to the union of certain coordinate hyperplanes.
It follows that the property (1) of the lemma holds on
(U_{x},G_{x})
.

We hence obtain a family of orbifold charts
\{(U_{x},G_{x})\}
by considering all points
x\in X_{i}
and all
i\in I
.
Since
S
is compact, we can extract a finite subset of this family, so that the images of the orbifold charts cover
S
.
Replacing
(X_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
by this finite family, and replacing
X
by the union of the images of these charts,
we may assume that the following property holds for the complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{X}=(X_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
.
There is an integer
N>0
, such that Proposition
5.2
holds for each
H_{i}\subseteq S_{i}\subseteq X_{i}
, with
N
and without shrinking
S_{i}
.
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

In the next lemma, we apply Proposition
5.2
on each orbifold chart
X_{i}
above.

Lemma 7.5
.

With the notation of Setup
7.2
,
we assume the properties of Lemma
7.4
hold.
Then there is a projective bimeromorphic morphism
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
such that the following properties hold.

(1)

There is a
f
-exceptional and
f
-ample divisor.

(2)

The indeterminacy locus of
f^{-1}
is a proper closed subset of
S
.
In particular, we can define the strict transform
S_{Y}
of
S
in
Y
, and the preiamge
H_{Y}
of
H
in
S_{Y}
under the morphism
f|_{S_{Y}}\colon S_{Y}\rightarrow S
.

(3)

Up to refining
I
, there is a complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{Y}=(Y_{i},\overline{G}_{i})_{i\in I}
whose quotient space is an open neighborhood of
S_{Y}
in
Y
.

(4)

Let
(S_{Y})_{i}
and
(H_{Y})_{i}
be the preimages of
S_{Y}
and
H_{Y}
respectively in
Y_{i}
.
Then
H_{Y}
induces a snc orbi-divisor on the complex orbifold
((S_{Y})_{i},\overline{G}_{i})
.
There is some open neighborhood
U_{i}
of
(S_{Y})_{i}
in
Y_{i}
,
such that
U_{i}
is isomorphic to an open neighborhood of
\{\mathbf{0}_{3}\}\times(S_{Y})_{i}

in the hypersurface in
\mathbb{\mathbb{D}}^{3}\times(S_{Y})_{i}
defined by a function as in Proposition
5.2
with respect to the pair
((S_{Y})_{i},(H_{Y})_{i})
.

(5)

Let
\Delta_{Y}
be the exceptional locus of
f
, let
(\Delta_{Y})_{i}
be its preimage in
Y_{i}
and
let
U^{\prime}_{i}=\cap_{g\in\overline{G}_{i}}g(U_{i})
.
Then
U^{\prime}_{i}
,
(\Delta_{Y})_{i}
,
(S_{Y})_{i}
,
(H_{Y})_{i}
and
\overline{G}_{i}
satisfy the items (1) and (2) of Setup
6.6
.
Moreover, the collection
(U^{\prime}_{i},\overline{G}_{i})_{i\in I}
induces a complex analytic orbispace,
whose quotient space
U^{\prime}
is an open neighborhood of
S_{Y}
.

(6)

The divisorial critical locus of
Y_{i}\rightarrow Y
is contained in
\Delta_{Y}
.

Proof.

We will first perform the construction of Proposition
5.2
on
X_{i}
.
Let
D^{1},...,D^{k}
be the irreducible components of
H
.
By the item (3) of Lemma
7.3
,
H\subseteq S
induces a snc orbi-divisor on the complex orbifold
(S_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
.
Hence for each irreducible component
D^{j}
, its preimage
D_{i}^{j}
in
X_{i}
is smooth.
Since any two irreducible components of
H_{i}
intersect by the item (1) of Lemma
7.4
, we deduce that if
D^{j}_{i}
is not empty, then it is an irreducible component of
H_{i}
.

Case 1.

We assume that the singularities of
X_{i}
at
S_{i}\setminus H_{i}
are of
A
-type or of
E
-type.
Then for each
i\in I
, we perform the following sequence of blowups on
X_{i}
,

Z_{i}=X_{i,k}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow X_{i,1}\rightarrow X_{i,0}=X_{i}

where
X_{i,j+1}\rightarrow X_{i,j}
is the blowup of
X_{i,j}
at
D^{j+1}_{i}
for
N
times,
as in Subsection
3.C
.
Then there is a natural action of
G_{i}
on
Y_{i}
.
Moreover, the family
(Z_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
induces a complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{Z}
with quotient space
Y
.
If
\psi_{i}\colon Z_{i}\rightarrow X_{i}
is the composition of the sequence above, then the collection
(\psi_{i})_{i\in I}

induces a proper bimeromorphic morphism
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
.
In addition, since
\psi_{i}
is the composition of blowups whose centers are compatible along the overlaps,
we deduce that there is some
\psi_{i}
-ample and
\psi_{i}
-exceptional divisor which is compatible along the overlaps.
Hence there is a
f
-exceptional and
f
-ample divisor.
Let
(S_{Z})_{i}
be the strict transform of
S_{i}
in
Z_{i}
.
By construction, there is some open neighborhood
U_{i}
of
(S_{Z})_{i}
, such that
U_{i}
is isomorphic to an open neighborhood of
\{\mathbf{0}_{3}\}\times(S_{Z})_{i}
in the hypersurface in
\mathbb{\mathbb{D}}^{3}\times(S_{Z})_{i}
defined by a function as in Proposition
5.2
.
Since the intersection of the divisorial critical locus of
X_{i}\rightarrow X
and
S
is contained in
H
, up to enlarging
N
, we can assume that the divisorial critical locus of
Z_{i}\rightarrow Y
is contained in
\Delta_{Y}
.
We set
\widehat{G}_{i}=G_{i}
and
\widehat{V}_{i}=X_{i}
in this case.

Case 2.

We assume that the singularities of
X_{i}
at
S_{i}\setminus H_{i}
are of
D
-type.
We will proceed as in Lemma
5.9
on each
X_{i}
.
Let
\varphi_{i}\colon W_{i}\rightarrow X_{i}
be the composition of blowups at the centers
D^{1}_{i},...,D^{k}_{i}
successively.
Then there is a natural action of
G_{i}
on
W_{i}
and
(W_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
induces a complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{W}
with quotient space
W
.
Furthermore, there is an induced projective bimeromorphic morphism
\varphi\colon W\rightarrow X
with a

\varphi
-ample and
\varphi
-exceptional divisor.
Let
S_{W}
be the strict transform of
S
in
W
,
and let
(S_{W})_{i}
be the strict transforms of
S_{i}
in
W_{i}
.
Let
H_{W}
be the preimage of
H
in
S_{W}
, and let
(H_{W})_{i}
the preimage of
H_{W}
in
W_{i}
.
Let
\mathcal{I}(D^{j}_{i})
be the ideal sheaf of
D^{j}_{i}
in
X_{i}
.
There is an open neighborhood
V_{i}
of
(S_{W})_{i}
such that
\varphi_{i}^{-1}\mathcal{I}(D^{j}_{i})\cdot\mathcal{O}_{V_{i}}
is generated by a holomorphic function
\sigma^{j}
on
V_{i}
, for
j=1,...,k
, see Remark
3.5
and Remark
5.10
.
Shrinking
V_{i}
we may assume that it is
G_{i}
-invariant.
We note that for every
j=1,...,k
, the collection of subspaces
(D^{j}_{i})_{i\in I}
is compatible along the overlaps.
By applying Lemma
2.13
for
k
times,
up to shrinking
V_{i}
and up to refining the family
I
,
we can assume the following properties.
Let
\widehat{V}_{i}=V_{i}[\sqrt[6]{\sigma_{1}},...,\sqrt[6]{\sigma_{k}}]
.
Then the morphism
\widehat{V}_{i}\rightarrow Z
is finite and Galois over its image.
Let
\widehat{G}_{i}
be its Galois group.
The collection
(\widehat{V_{i}},\widehat{G_{i}})_{i\in I}
induces a complex analytic orbispace

\widehat{\mathfrak{V}}
,
whose quotient space
\widehat{V}
is an open neighborhood of
S_{W}
in
W
.
Let
(S_{\widehat{V}})_{i}
be the preimage of
(S_{W})_{i}
in
\widehat{V}_{i}
.
Then the finite morphism
(S_{\widehat{V}})_{i}\rightarrow(S_{W})_{i}
is a cyclic cover,
branched over the components of
(H_{W})_{i}
.
Indeed,
(S_{\widehat{V}})_{i}
is also isomorphic to a contractible neighborhood of the origin of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
, and the preimage
(H_{\widehat{V}})_{i}
of
(H_{W})_{i}
in

(S_{\widehat{V}})_{i}
is isomorphic to the union of some coordinate hyperplanes.
In particular,
H_{W}
induces a snc orbi-divisor on the complex orbifold
((S_{\widehat{V}})_{i},\widehat{G}_{i})_{i\in I}
.

Now, as in the Case 1, we blow up
\widehat{V_{i}}
at the preimage of each irreducible component of
H_{W}
for
N
times as in Subsection
3.C
.
We obtain a complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{Z}=(Z_{i},\widehat{G}_{i})
with quotient space
Y^{\prime}
.
Let
(S_{Z})_{i}
be the strict transform of
(S_{\widehat{V}})_{i}
in
Z_{i}
.
By the construction of Lemma
5.9
,
there is some open neighborhood
U_{i}
of
(S_{Z})_{i}
in
Z_{i}
, such that

U_{i}
is isomorphic to an open neighborhood of
\{\mathbf{0}_{3}\}\times(S_{Z})_{i}

in the hypersurface in
\mathbb{\mathbb{D}}^{3}\times(S_{Z})_{i}
defined by a function as in Proposition
5.2
with respect to the pair
((S_{Z})_{i},(H_{Z})_{i})
.
Furthermore, there is a proper bimeromorphic morphism
h\colon Y^{\prime}\rightarrow\widehat{V}
with some
h
-ample and
h
-exceptional divisor.
Since the indeterminacy locus of
h^{-1}
is contained in the compact set
S_{W}
, we deduce that
h
extends to a projective bimeromorphic morphism
h\colon Y\rightarrow W
by Lemma
2.3
.
Hence we obtain a projective bimeromorphic morphism
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
, with a
f
-exceptional and
f
-ample divisor.
Furthermore, up to enlarging
N
,
we can assume that the divisorial critical locus of
Z_{i}\rightarrow Y
is contained in
\Delta_{Y}
.

In both cases, we have constructed a projective bimeromorphic morphism
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
, and a complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{Z}=(Z_{i},\widehat{G}_{i})
,
whose quotient space, denoted by
Z
, is an open neighborhood of the strict transform
S_{Y}
of
S
in
Y
.
By construction, the items (1) and (2) of the lemma hold for
f
.
Furthermore, the items (3) and (4) hold for
\mathfrak{Z}=(Z_{i},\widehat{G}_{i})
.
However, in the item (5), there is an issue which is the item (2) of Setup
6.6
.
To handle it, we apply the method of cyclic covers as in Lemma
6.13
.
Since
\psi_{i}\colon Z_{i}\rightarrow\widehat{V}_{i}
is obtained by blowing up the components
(D_{\widehat{V}})_{i}^{j}
of
(H_{\widehat{V}})_{i}
,
we can proceed as in the first paragraph of the Case 2 above.
By Lemma
2.13
, up to refining the family
I
,
there is a
\widehat{G}_{i}
-invariant neighborhood
V^{\prime}_{i}
of
(S_{Z})_{i}
in
Z_{i}
, and a cyclic covering space

Y_{i}=V^{\prime}_{i}[\sqrt[6]{\delta_{1}},...,\sqrt[6]{\delta_{k}}]
,
such that
\delta_{j}
is a generator of
\psi_{i}^{-1}\mathcal{I}((D_{\widehat{V}})_{i}^{j})\cdot\mathcal{O}_{V^{\prime}_{i}}
,
and that
Y_{i}\rightarrow V^{\prime}_{i}/\widehat{G}_{i}
is Galois of group
\overline{G}_{i}
.
Then the collection
(Y_{i},\overline{G}_{i})_{i\in I}
defines a complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{Y}
,
whose quotient space is an open neighborhood of
S_{Y}
.
Moreover, by Lemma
6.13
and Remark
3.5
,
the item (4) holds on
Y_{i}
,
and the vanishing orders of the functions
\alpha
and
\beta
in the defining function of
U_{i}
in
\mathbb{D}^{3}\times(S_{Y})_{i}
are divisible by 6 along each component of
(H_{Y})_{i}
.
We notice that the item (1) of Setup
6.6
is satisfied by the construction of
U_{i}^{\prime}
.
Hence we obtain the item (5) of the present lemma.

The divisorial critical locus of
Y_{i}\rightarrow V^{\prime}_{i}
is contained in the
\psi_{i}
-exceptional locus, hence is contained in the preimages of
\Delta_{Y}
in
V^{\prime}_{i}
.
Since the divisorial critical locus of
V^{\prime}_{i}\rightarrow Y
is contained in
\Delta_{Y}
,
we deduce the item (6).
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

The following lemma will be applied on the neighborhood
U^{\prime}
of
S_{Y}
in the item (5) of Lemma
7.5
.

Lemma 7.6
.

With the notation of Setup
7.2
,
let
\Delta
be a reduced divisor in
X
and let
\Delta_{i}
be its preimage in
X_{i}
.
Suppose that
\Delta
contains the divisorial critical locus of
X_{i}\rightarrow X
,
and that
H
induces a snc orbi-divisor on the complex orbifold
(S_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
.
Assume that the data
X_{i}
,
\Delta_{i}
,
S_{i}
,
H_{i}
and
G_{i}
satisfy the items (1) and (2) of Setup
6.6
.
Then, there is a projective bimeromorphic morphism
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
with
Y
normal such that the following properties hold.

(1)

There is a
f
-exceptional and
f
-ample divisor.

(2)

The indeterminacy locus of
f^{-1}
is a proper closed subset of
S
.
In particular, we can define the strict transform
S_{Y}
of
S
in
Y
, and the preiamge
H_{Y}
of
H
in
S_{Y}
under the morphism
f|_{S_{Y}}\colon S_{Y}\rightarrow S
.

(3)

There is a complex analytic orbispace
\overline{\mathfrak{W}}=(\overline{W}_{k},\overline{G}_{k},\psi_{k})_{k\in K}
,
whose quotient space
W
is an open neighborhood of
S_{Y}
in
Y
.

(4)

The divisorial critical locus of
\psi_{k}\colon\overline{W}_{k}\rightarrow W
is contained in
\Delta_{Y}
, where
\Delta_{Y}=f^{-1}(\Delta)
.

(5)

\overline{W}_{k}
is smooth on
\psi_{k}^{-1}(W\setminus\Delta_{Y})
.

Proof.

We will apply Proposition
6.7
on each
X_{i}
.
Since the construction is local around a point,
there may be infinitely many constructions to consider.
However, since
S
is compact,
we can reduce to applying the construction for only finitely times.

More concretely, for each point
x\in S_{i}
, we let
S_{x}
be an open neighborhood of it as the one in the item (4) of Setup
6.6
.
In particular, if
(G_{i})_{x}\subseteq G_{i}
is the subgroup which fixes
x
,
then
(S_{x},(G_{i})_{x})
is an orbifold chart of
\pi_{i}(S_{x})
, compatible with the orbifold
(S_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
.
There is a positive integer
N_{x}
,
such that Proposition
6.7
holds on
X_{i}
with respect to
S_{x}
and
N_{x}
.
Since
S
is compact, by considering
S_{x}
for all
x\in S_{i}
and all
i\in I
,
we can obtain a finite family
(x_{k})_{k\in K}
of points and an application
\sigma\colon K\rightarrow I
such that the following properties hold.
Each point
x_{k}
belongs to
S_{\sigma(k)}
and

\bigcup_{k\in K}\pi_{\sigma(k)}(S_{x_{k}})=S.

Since
K
is finite, we can define the positive integer
N:=\operatorname{max}\{N_{x_{k}}\ |\ k\in K\}
.

Let
D^{1},...,D^{e}
be the irreducible components of
H
.
Since
H
induces a snc orbi-divisor on the complex analytic orbispace
(S_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
, if the preimage
D^{j}_{i}
of
D^{j}
in
X_{i}
is not empty,
then it is smooth and hence irreducible.
Let
f_{i}\colon Y_{i}\rightarrow X_{i}
be the morphism obtained by
blowing up of
X_{i}
at
D^{j}_{i}
for
6N
times, successively for
j=1,...,e
, as in Subsection
3.C
.
Then there is a natural action of
G_{i}
on
Y_{i}
and the collection
(Y_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
induces a complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{Y}
with quotient space
Y
.
Furthermore, there is an induced projective bimeromorphic morphism
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
with a
f
-ample and
f
-exceptional divisor.
Let
S_{Y}
be the strict transform of
S
in
Y
.

From the choice of
N
, for each
k\in K
, there is an open neighborhood
W_{k}
of the strict transform of
S_{x_{k}}
in
Y_{\sigma(k)}
, which is invariant under
G_{k}:=(G_{\sigma(k)})_{x_{k}}
,
such that there is a finite cover
\nu_{k}\colon\overline{W}_{k}\rightarrow W_{k}
satisfying the conclusions of Proposition
6.7
.
The property (4) of Proposition
6.7
implies that the natural morphism
\psi_{k}\colon\overline{W}_{k}\rightarrow Y

is finite and Galois of group
\overline{G}_{k}
over the normalization of its image.
We remark that, up to shrinking
W_{k}
around
S_{x_{k}}
, we can assume that
(W_{k},G_{k})
is an orbifold chart over
Y
compatible with
\mathfrak{Y}
.

Let
W
be the union of the images of the
W_{k}
’s in
Y
.
Then
W
is the quotient space of the complex analytic orbispace
(W_{k},G_{k})_{k\in K}
and is an open neighborhood of
S_{Y}
.
By Lemma
4.8
, up to shrinking
W_{k}
, we can assume that
W_{k}
is locally irreducible.
Hence
W
is locally irreducible as well.
Thus the normalization morphism
W^{\prime}\rightarrow W
is a homeomorphism, and we may identify
W^{\prime}
and
W
as the same topological space.
We will show that
(\overline{W}_{k},\overline{G}_{k})_{k\in K}
induces a complex analytic orbispace, whose quotient space is
W^{\prime}
.

Let
w\in\psi_{k}(\overline{W}_{k})\cap\psi_{l}(\overline{W}_{l})\subseteq W
for some
k,l\in K
.
Then

f(w)\in\pi_{\sigma(k)}(X_{\sigma(k)})\cap\pi_{\sigma(l)}(X_{\sigma(l)})\subseteq X.

Hence there is an open neighborhood
X^{\prime}
of
f(w)
in
X
, and an orbifold chart
(Z,G^{\prime})
of
X^{\prime}
, such that there are inclusions of orbifold charts
\zeta_{k}\colon Z\rightarrow X_{\sigma(k)}
and
\zeta_{l}\colon Z\rightarrow X_{\sigma(l)}
.
Let
(Z_{Y},G^{\prime})
be the orbifold chart on
f^{-1}(X^{\prime})
induced by
(Z,G^{\prime})
,
which is compatible with
\mathfrak{Y}
.
Then there is an open neighborhood
U
of
w
in
\psi_{k}(\overline{W}_{k})\cap\psi_{l}(\overline{W}_{l})
, and an orbifold chart
(V,G)
over
U
, such that there are inclusions of orbifold charts

\iota_{k}\colon V\rightarrow W_{k},\ \iota_{l}\colon V\rightarrow W_{l}\mbox{ and }V\rightarrow Z_{Y}.

Up to shrinking
U
, we may assume that
w
has a unique preiamge
o
in
V
.
We set

\overline{V}_{k}=\nu_{k}^{-1}(\iota_{k}(V))\subseteq\overline{W}_{k}\mbox{ and }\overline{V}_{l}=\nu_{l}^{-1}(\iota_{l}(V))\subseteq\overline{W}_{l}.

Then the finite morphism
\overline{V}_{k}\rightarrow\iota_{k}(V)
is constructed by applying Proposition
6.7
on
\zeta_{k}(Z)\subseteq X_{\sigma(k)}
,
and
\overline{V}_{l}\rightarrow\iota_{l}(V_{l})
is constructed by applying Proposition
6.7
on
\zeta_{l}(Z)\subseteq X_{\sigma(l)}
.
By the property (5) of Proposition
6.7
, we deduce that, up to shrinking
U
,
every connected component of
\overline{V}_{k}
is isomorphic to every connected component of
\overline{V}_{l}
.
Lemma
2.11
then
implies that
(\overline{W}_{k},\overline{G}_{k})_{k\in K}
induces a complex analytic orbispace
\overline{\mathfrak{W}}
.
Its quotient space is exactly
W^{\prime}
since
W^{\prime}
and every
\overline{W}_{k}
are normal.
Replacing
Y
by its normalization, we can assume that
W^{\prime}=W
, which is an open neighborhood of
S_{Y}
in
Y
.

It remains to prove the items (4) and (5).
We notice that the divisorial critical locus of
W_{k}\rightarrow W
is contained in
\Delta_{Y}
, as the divisorial critical locus of
X_{\sigma(k)}\rightarrow X
is contained in
\Delta
.
Since the divisorial locus of
\overline{W}_{k}\rightarrow W_{k}
is contained in the preimage of
\Delta_{Y}
by construction, we deduce the item (4).
The item (5) follows from the item (3) of Proposition
6.7
.
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Now we can complete the proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}=(X_{k},G_{k})_{k\in K}
be the complex analytic orbispace induced by the index-one covers of
\omega_{X}
.
Then
X_{k}\rightarrow X
is quasi-étale onto its image, and
X_{k}
has at most canonical singularities.
Let
S_{k}
be the preimage of
S
in
X_{k}
.
We note that
S_{k}
may not be irreducible.
Let
h_{k}\colon X^{\prime}_{k}\rightarrow X_{k}
be the bimeromorphic projective morphism induced by the functorial desingularization of
S_{k}
, see the beginning of Subsection
3.B
.
By construction, the strict trasnform
S^{\prime}_{k}=(h_{k}^{-1})_{*}S_{k}
is smooth.
There is a natural action of
G_{k}
on
X^{\prime}_{k}
and we obtain a complex analytic orbispace

\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}=(X^{\prime}_{k},G_{k},\mu_{k})_{k\in K}
with quotient space
X^{\prime}
.
Furthermore, there is a natural projective bimeromorphic morphism
h\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
, with a
h
-ample and
h
-exceptional divisor.
We remark that the indeterminacy locus of
h^{-1}
is a proper closed subset of
S
.
Moreover, the divisorial critical locus of
\mu_{k}
is contained in the
h
-exceptional locus.

First we assume that
X_{k}
is smooth at general points of the components of
S_{k}
.
Then
X^{\prime}_{k}
is smooth around general points of the components of
S^{\prime}_{k}
.
Let
Y_{k}\rightarrow X^{\prime}_{k}
be the projective bimeromorphic morphism as in Corollary
4.6
, with respect to
S^{\prime}_{k}
.
If
(S_{Y})_{k}
is the strict transform of
S^{\prime}_{k}
in
Y_{k}
, then
Y_{k}
is smooth around
(S_{Y})_{k}
.
Up to taking the normalization, we can assume that every
Y_{k}
is normal.
Since the constructions are functorial, there is a natural action of
G_{k}
on
Y_{k}
,
and we have a complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{Y}=(Y_{k},G_{k},\psi_{k})_{k\in K}
with quotient space
Y
, which is normal.
Furthermore, there is a natural projective bimeromorphic morphism
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
.
Since
Y_{k}
is smooth around
(S_{Y})_{k}
, there is a smooth
G_{k}
-invariant open neighborhood
W_{k}
of
(S_{Y})_{k}
.
Then
(W_{k},G_{k},\psi_{k})_{k\in K}
is a complex orbifold, whose quotient space is an open neighborhood
W
of
S_{Y}
.
Hence
f
satisfies the properties of the proposition.

In the remainder of the proof, we assume that the irreducible components of
S_{k}
are irreducible components of the singular locus of
X_{k}
.
Let
S^{\prime}
be the strict transform of
S
in
X^{\prime}
.
Then
\mu_{k}^{-1}(S^{\prime})=S_{k}^{\prime}
is smooth.
Therefore, for a point
o\in S^{\prime}
, there is an orbifold chart
(U_{o},G_{o})
over a neighborhood of
o
in
X^{\prime}
, compatible with the complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}
, such that the preiamge of
S^{\prime}
in
U_{o}
is an irreducible component of the singular locus of
U_{o}
.
Since
S^{\prime}
is compact, by considering the
(U_{o},G_{o})
’s for all points
o\in S^{\prime}
, we obtain a complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{Z}=(Z_{j},G^{\prime}_{j},\nu_{j})_{j\in J}
with a finite family
J
, whose quotient space is an open neighborhood
Z
of
S^{\prime}
in
X^{\prime}
.
Moreover, each orbifold chart
(Z_{j},G^{\prime}_{j})
is compatible with
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}
.
There is a proper closed subset
H^{\prime}\subseteq S^{\prime}
, such that
Z_{j}
has the same type of canonical singularities at points of
\nu_{j}^{-1}(S^{\prime})\setminus\nu_{j}^{-1}(H^{\prime})
.
We can pick
H^{\prime}
so that it contains the intersection of
S^{\prime}
and the
h
-exceptional locus.
It follows that the intersection of the divisorial critical locus of
\nu_{j}
and
S^{\prime}
is contained in
H^{\prime}
.
Therefore the data
Z
,
\mathfrak{Z}
,
S^{\prime}
and
H^{\prime}
satisfy the conditions of Setup
7.2
.

We apply Lemma
7.3
on
Z
and obtain a projective bimeromorphic morphism

h_{1}\colon Z_{1}\rightarrow Z
.
Since the indeterminacy locus of
h_{1}^{-1}
is contained in
S^{\prime}
, which is compact,
we deduce from Lemma
2.3
that
h_{1}
extends to a proper bimeromorphic morphism
Y_{1}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
.
Let
f_{1}\colon Y_{1}\rightarrow X
be the natural morphism.
Then there is a
f_{1}
-ample and
f_{1}
-exceptional divisor.
Let
S_{Y_{1}}
be the strict transform of
S^{\prime}
in
Y_{1}
, and let
H_{Y_{1}}
be the reduced divisor in
S_{Y_{1}}
as in the item (3) of Lemma
7.3
.
By applying Lemma
7.4
around
S_{Y_{1}}
, we can obtain an open neighborhood
Q_{1}
of it, so that we can apply Lemma
7.5
to
Q_{1}
.
Then we obtain a projective bimeromorphic morphism

g_{2}\colon P_{2}\rightarrow Q_{1}
.
By Lemma
2.3
again,
g_{2}
extends to a projective bimeromorphic morphism
g_{2}\colon Y_{2}\rightarrow Y_{1}
.
Let
f_{2}\colon Y_{2}\rightarrow X
be the induced morphism.

Let
S_{Y_{2}}
be the strict transform of
S_{Y_{1}}
in
Y_{2}
,
let
Q_{2}
be the open neighborhood of
S_{Y_{2}}
as in the item (5) of
Lemma
7.5
, and let
\Delta_{2}
be the whole reduced
g_{2}
-exceptional divisor.
We remark that the intersection of the
f_{1}
-exceptional set and
S_{Y_{1}}
is contained in
H_{Y_{1}}
.
Thus, up to blowing up more times in the construction of Lemma
7.5
,
we can assume that the strict transforms of the
f_{1}
-exceptional divisors in
Y_{2}
are disjoint from
Q_{2}
.
In particular, if
\Gamma_{2}
is the whole reduced
f_{2}
-exceptional divisor, then
\Gamma_{2}\cap Q_{2}=\Delta_{2}\cap Q_{2}
.

We can apply Lemma
7.6
to
Q_{2}
with the divisor
\Gamma_{2}\cap Q_{2}=\Delta_{2}\cap Q_{2}
, and obtain a projective bimeromorphic morphism

g_{3}\colon P_{3}\rightarrow Q_{2}
.
By Lemma
2.3
, we deduce that
g_{3}
extends to a projective bimeromorphic morphism
g_{3}\colon Y_{3}\rightarrow Y_{2}
.
Let
Y=Y_{3}
and let
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
be the natural morphism.
Then
f
satisfies all the properties of the proposition.
∎

8.
Proof of the modification theorem

We will complete the proof of Theorem
1.2
in this section.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
X
be a normal complex analytic variety, let
S\subseteq X_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sing}}}
be a compact irreducible component of codimension
2
, and let
\Delta
be a reduced divisor in
X
.
Suppose that
\Delta
does not contain
S
.
Assume that there is a normal complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{W}=(W_{i},G_{i},\psi_{i})_{i\in I}

satisfying the following properties.

(1)

The quotient space
W
of
\mathfrak{W}
is an open neighborhood of
S
in
X
.

(2)

The divisorial critical locus of
\psi_{i}\colon W_{i}\rightarrow W
is contained in
\Delta
.

(3)

W_{i}
is smooth on
\psi_{i}^{-1}(W\setminus\Delta)
.

Assume that there is a projective bimeromorphic morphism
h\colon(Z,\Gamma)\rightarrow(X,\Delta)
satisfying the following properties.
The divisor
\Gamma
is the sum of
h^{-1}_{*}\Delta
and the whole reduced
h
-exceptional divisor.
The
h
-exceptional locus is contained in
\Gamma
.
The pair
(Z,\Gamma)
is dlt and each irreducible component of
\Gamma
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier.
The morphism
h
is an isomorphism
over general points of
S
.
The pair
(Z,\Gamma)
is snc on
h^{-1}(X\setminus S)
.

Then there is a projective bimeromorphic morphism
\varphi\colon Y\rightarrow Z
with a
\varphi
-ample and
\varphi
-exceptional divisor, such that
Y
has quotient singularities. Furthermore,
\varphi
is an isomorphism over
h^{-1}(X\setminus S)
and over general points of the strict transform of
S
in
Z
.

Proof.

Let
V=h^{-1}(W)
and let
V_{i}
be the normalization of the main component of
W_{i}\times_{W}V
.
Then there is a natural action of
G_{i}
on
V_{i}
.

\textstyle{V_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{i}}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{W_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{i}}
\textstyle{W}

Since
V
is normal, the family
(V_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
induces a complex analytic orbispace whose quotient space is
V
.
Since
Z\setminus\Gamma
is isomorphic to an open subset of
X\setminus\Delta
,
we deduce from the condition (2) that,
if
\mu_{i}\colon V_{i}\rightarrow V
is the natural morphism,
then its divisorial critical locus is contained in
\Gamma
.
In addition, the condition (3) implies that

V_{i}
is smooth on
\mu_{i}^{-1}(V\setminus\Gamma)
.

Let
S_{Z}
be the strict transform of
S
in
Z
.
Let
\Gamma^{j}
with
j=1,...,l
be the irreducible components of
\Gamma
, and let
r^{j}
be the Cartier index of
\Gamma^{j}
.
For every point
z\in h^{-1}(S)
, there is an open neighborhood
T
of
z
in
V
such that each

r^{j}\Gamma^{j}|_{T}
is defined by some holomorphic function
\sigma^{j}
.
Thus
we obtain a family
(U_{k})_{k\in K}
of open subsets of
V
,
whose union contains
h^{-1}(S)
,
such that
r^{j}\Gamma^{j}|_{U_{k}}
is connected and defined by some holomorphic function
\sigma^{j}_{k}
.
Furthermore, there is an application
\iota\colon K\rightarrow I
such that

U_{k}
is contained in
\mu_{\iota(k)}(V_{\iota(k)})
.
By convention, we set
\sigma_{k}^{j}=1
if
\Gamma^{j}|_{U_{k}}
is empty.
Let
U=\bigcup_{k\in K}U_{k}
.
Then it is a neighborhood of
h^{-1}(S)
in
V
.

We notice that, since
(Z,\Gamma)
is dlt, each
\Gamma^{j}
is normal.
It follows that if
\Gamma^{j}|_{U_{k}}
is not empty, then it is normal and hence irreducible.
In particular, we can apply the result from Lemma
2.6
to Lemma
2.8
on each pair
(U_{k},\Gamma|_{U_{k}})
.
Let
M>0
be an integer which is divisible by the degrees of all the
\mu_{i}
’s.
We define
\overline{U}_{k}
as a connected component of

U_{k}[\sqrt[Mr^{1}]{\sigma_{k}^{1}},...,\sqrt[Mr^{l}]{\sigma_{k}^{l}}]^{nor}

and we let
\xi_{k}\colon\overline{U}_{k}\rightarrow U_{k}
be the natural morphism.
Then the
\overline{U}_{k}
’s define a normal complex analytic orbispace with quotient space
U
, by Lemma
2.6
and Lemma
2.11
.
Let
Q_{k}
be a connected component of the normalization of
\overline{U}_{k}\times_{Z}V_{\iota(k)}
.
It follows that the natural finite morphism
Q_{k}\rightarrow U_{k}
is Galois of Galois group
H_{k}
, and the collection
(Q_{k},H_{k})_{k\in K}
induces a normal complex analytic orbispace
\mathfrak{Q}
with quotient space
U
.

\textstyle{Q_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{k}}
\textstyle{V_{\iota(k)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{U}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{k}}
\textstyle{U_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z}

We denote by
\eta_{k}\colon Q_{k}\rightarrow\overline{U}_{k}
the natural morphism.
Then by Lemma
2.8
, we obtain that
\eta_{k}
is quasi-étale;
furthermore,
Q_{k}
is smooth in codimension 2 since
V_{\iota(k)}
is smooth over
U_{k}\setminus\Gamma
.

The Zariski’s purity theorem implies that

(Q_{k})_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sing}}}\subseteq(\eta_{k})^{-1}((\overline{U}_{k})_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sing}}}).

Since
(Z,\Gamma)
is snc on
Z\setminus h^{-1}(S)
, we deduce that
\overline{U}_{k}
is smooth over
Z\setminus h^{-1}(S)
by Lemma
2.7
.
It follows that

(Q_{k})_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sing}}}\subseteq(\xi_{k}\circ\eta_{k})^{-1}(h^{-1}(S)).

Let
\widetilde{Q}_{k}\rightarrow Q_{k}
be the functorial desingularization, which is an isomorphism over the smooth locus of
Q_{k}
.
Then there is a natural action of
H_{k}
on
\widetilde{Q}_{k}
, and we obtain a complex orbifold

\widetilde{\mathfrak{Q}}=(\widetilde{Q}_{k},H_{k})_{k\in K}
with quotient space
\widetilde{Q}
.
In particular,
\widetilde{Q}
has quotient singularities only.
There is an induced projective bimeromorphic morphism
\varphi\colon\widetilde{Q}\rightarrow U
.
From the properties of
(Q_{k})_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sing}}}
above, we see that the indeterminacy locus of
\varphi^{-1}
is contained in
h^{-1}(S)
and is of codimension at least 3 in
Z
.
In particular,
\varphi
is an isomorphism over general points of
S_{Z}
.
Furthermore, there is an
\varphi
-exceptional and
\varphi
-ample divisor.
Thus by Lemma
2.3
,
\varphi
extends to a projective bimeromorphic morphism

\varphi\colon Y\rightarrow Z
.
Since
Z
is smooth outside
h^{-1}(S)
, we deduce that
Y
has quotient singularities only.
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Now we can deduce Theorem
1.2
.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

We will divide the proof into several steps.
Let
S_{e}
with
e=1,...,r
be all the irreducible components of
X_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sing}}}
which have codimension 2 in
X
.

Step 1.
In this step, we will reduce to the case when
S_{1},...,S_{r}
are pairwise disjoint.
By blowing up at centers contained in at least two of the
S_{e}
’s,
we obtain a projective bimeromorphic morphism
h\colon Z\rightarrow X
such that the strict transforms
h^{-1}_{*}S_{1},...,h^{-1}_{*}S_{r}
are pairwise disjoint.
Let
X^{\prime}\rightarrow Z
be the projective bimeromorphic morphism constructed in Lemma
2.18
, with respect to the collection
\{h^{-1}_{*}S_{1},...,h^{-1}_{*}S_{r}\}
.
We observe that the indeterminacy locus of
X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
has codimension at least 3.
Up to replacing
X
by
X^{\prime}
, we may assume that
S_{1},...,S_{r}
are pairwise disjoint.
Furthermore,
X
has klt singularities.

Step 2.
In this step, we will reduce to the situation of Lemma
8.1
.
Let
X_{e}
be an open neighborhood of
S_{e}
in
X
such that the
X_{e}\cap X_{e^{\prime}}=\emptyset
if
e\neq e^{\prime}
.
We apply Proposition
7.1
to each
X_{e}
.
Then we have projective bimeromorphic morphisms
p_{e}\colon X^{\prime}_{e}\rightarrow X_{e}
, which satisfies the properties of Proposition
7.1
.
Since for each
e
, there is a
p_{e}
-ample and
p_{e}
-exceptional divisor, and since the indeterminacy locus of
(p_{e})^{-1}
is contained in
S_{e}
and hence compact,
by applying Lemma
2.3
for several times, there is a projective bimeromorphic morphism
p\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
such that

p=p_{e}
over
X_{e}
.
Let
\Delta
be the whole reduced
p
-exceptional divisor.
Then
p(\Delta)
has codimension at least 3 in
X
.

In the remainder of the proof, by abuse of notation, we denote
X^{\prime}
by
X
, and
X_{e}^{\prime}
by
X_{e}
.
If
\Delta_{e}=\Delta|_{X_{e}}
, then
X_{e}
,
\Delta_{e}
and
S_{e}
satisfy the conditions (1)-(3) of Lemma
8.1
.
Furthermore, from now on, it is enough to construct
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
, such that
Y
has quotient singularities,
and
f
is an isomorphism over general points of each
S_{e}
and over
X_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sm}}}\setminus\Delta
.
We remark that
X_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sm}}}\setminus\Delta
is contained in the snc locus of
(X,\Delta)
.

Step 3.
In this step, we will construct a bimeromorphic morphism
h\colon Z\rightarrow X
,
which plays the same role for
X_{e}

as
Z
for
X
in Lemma
8.1
.
We first note that, by the property (3) of Lemma
8.1
,
X
has quotient singularities around
S\setminus\Delta
,
where
S=\bigcup_{e=1}^{r}S_{e}
.
Thus, we can apply Corollary
3.4
to
(X,\Delta)
, with respect to
S
,
and obtain a projective bimeromorphic morphism
\rho\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
with
X^{\prime}
normal.
By construction,
\rho
is an isomorphism over the snc locus of
(X,\Delta)
and over general points of each
S_{e}
;
if
\Delta^{\prime}
is the sum of
\rho^{-1}_{*}\Delta
and the whole reduced
\rho
-exceptional divisor,
then
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is snc on
\rho^{-1}(X\setminus S)
;
furthermore,
\Delta^{\prime}
is the support of some Cartier divisor, and
X^{\prime}\setminus\Delta^{\prime}
has quotient singularities.
Let
S^{\prime}
be the strict transform of
S
in
X^{\prime}
.
Let
(Z,\Gamma)\rightarrow(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt modification as in Lemma
2.19
,
which is an isomorphism over the snc locus of
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
,
and over general points of each component of
S^{\prime}
.
We denote by
h\colon Z\rightarrow X
the natural morphism.
Then we have the following properties.
The divisor
\Gamma
is the sum of
h^{-1}_{*}\Delta
and the whole reduced
h
-exceptional divisor.
The
h
-exceptional locus is contained in
\Gamma
.
Each irreducible component of
\Gamma
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier.
The morphism
h
is an isomorphism over the snc locus of
(X,\Delta)
and over general points of each component of
S
.
The pair
(Z,\Gamma)
is snc on
h^{-1}(X\setminus S)
.

Step 4.
We complete the proof in this final step.
Let
Z_{e}=h^{-1}(X_{e})
and
\Gamma_{e}=\Gamma|_{Z_{e}}
.
Then

Z_{\operatorname{\textsubscript{\rm sing}}}\subseteq h^{-1}(S)\subseteq\bigcup_{e=1}^{r}Z_{e}.

We can apply Lemma
8.1
on each
X_{e}
, and obtain a projective bimeromorphic morphism
\varphi_{e}\colon Y_{e}\rightarrow Z_{e}
as in the Lemma
8.1
.
The indeterminacy locus of
(\varphi_{e})^{-1}
is contained in
h^{-1}(S_{e})
, hence is compact.
Since there is a
\varphi_{e}
-ample and
\varphi_{e}
-exceptional divisor, by applying Lemma
2.3
for several times,
we deduce that there is a projective bimeromorphic morphism
\varphi\colon Y\rightarrow Z
such that its restriction over
Z_{e}
is equal to
\varphi_{e}
,
and that it is an isomorphism elsewhere.
In particular,
Y
has quotient singularities.
Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
be natural morphism.
Then
f
is an isomorphism over the snc locus of
(X,\Delta)
and over general points of
S_{1},...,S_{r}
.
This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

9.
The Bogomolov-Gieseker inequality

In this section, we will prove Theorem
1.3
.
The notion of Kähler spaces was introduced in
[
Gra62
]
.
For Kähler orbifolds and orbifold coherent sheaves, see for example
[
Fau22
, Section 2]
,
[
Wu23
, Section 2]
or
[
DO23
, Section 3.1]
.

If
X
is a compact complex analytic variety of dimension
n
, then there is a natural isomorphism
H_{2n}(X,\mathbb{R})\cong H_{2n}^{BM}(X,\mathbb{R})
, where
H_{2n}
is the singular homology and
H_{2n}^{BM}
is the Borel-Moore homology.
We note that
H_{2n}^{BM}(X,\mathbb{R})\cong\mathbb{R}
and
it is generated by a canonical element
[X]
, called the fundamental class of
X
.
For more details on Borel-Moore homology, we refer to
[
Ful98
, Chapter 19]
and the references therein.
As a consequence, for cohomology classes
\sigma_{1},...,\sigma_{k}\in H^{\bullet}(X,\mathbb{R})
, such that the sum of their degrees is equal to
2n
, we can define the following intersection number,

\sigma_{1}\cdots\sigma_{k}:=(\sigma_{1}\smallsmile\cdots\smallsmile\sigma_{k})\smallfrown[X]\in\mathbb{R}.

When
X
has only quotient singularities, this intersection form can be identified with the orbifold Poincaré duality, see
[
Sat56
, Theorem 3]
.

9.A.
Orbifold Chern classes

We will explain the orbifold Chern classes
\hat{c}_{k}
for
k=1,2
in the statement of Theorem
1.3
.
We also refer to
[
GK20
, Section 5]
for a similar discussion.

Let
X
be a compact complex analytic variety of dimension
n
,
which has quotient singularities in codimension 2.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent reflexive sheaf on
X
.
Then there is a Zariski open subset
X^{\circ}
of
X
, whose complement has codimension at least 3, such that there is a standard complex orbifold
\mathfrak{X}=(X_{j},G^{\prime}_{j})
whose quotient space is
X^{\circ}
.
Being standard means that the action of
G^{\prime}_{j}
is free in codimension 1.
Furthermore, since reflexive sheaves on complex manifolds are locally free in codimension 2, up to shrinking
X^{\circ}
, we can assume that
\mathcal{F}
induces an orbifold vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{orb}}
on
\mathfrak{X}
, see
[
DO23
, Remark 3.4.(5)]
.

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
be an orbifold modification as in Theorem
1.2
.
Then
Y
has quotient singularities.
Let
\mathfrak{Y}=(Y_{i},G_{i})
be the standard orbifold structure on
Y
,
and let
\mathcal{E}=(f^{*}\mathcal{F})/(\mathrm{torsion})
.
Then
\mathcal{E}
induces a reflexive coherent orbifold sheaf
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{orb}}
on
\mathfrak{Y}
,
see for example
[
DO23
, Remark 3.4.(5)]
.
By shrinking
X^{\circ}
, we may assume that
f
is an isomorphism over it.

By
[
DO23
, Theorem 3.10]
, there is a functorial projective bimeromorphic morphism
p_{i}\colon Z_{i}\rightarrow Y_{i}
,
such that
Z_{i}
is smooth, that
\mathcal{H}_{i}:=p_{i}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{i}/(\mathrm{torsion})
is locally free, and that the indeterminacy locus of
p_{i}^{-1}
has codimension at least 3.
The functoriality implies that there is a canonical action of
G_{i}
on
Z_{i}
such that
p_{i}
is
G_{i}
-equivariant.
Furthermore, the
(Z_{i},G_{i})
’s induce a complex orbifold
\mathfrak{Z}
with quotient space
Z
.
The collection
(\mathcal{H}_{i})
defines an orbifold vector bundle
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}}
on
\mathfrak{Z}
.
Therefore, we have well-defined orbifold Chern classes
\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}})
and
\hat{c}_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}})
in
H^{\bullet}(Z,\mathbb{R})
, see
[
Bla96
, Section 2]
or
[
DO23
, Definition 3.3]
.
Let
q\colon Z\rightarrow X
be the natural morphism.
Then the indeterminacy locus of
q^{-1}
has codimension at least 3.
We can now define the orbifold Chern classes
\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{F})
,
\hat{c}_{2}(\mathcal{F})
and
\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{F})^{2}
.

Definition 9.1
.

The orbifold Chern class
\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{F})
,
\hat{c}_{2}(\mathcal{F})
and
\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{F})^{2}
are defined as linear forms on
H^{\bullet}(X,\mathbb{R})
, so that
for any class
\sigma\in H^{\bullet}(X,\mathbb{R})
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{c}_{k}(\mathcal{F})\cdot\sigma=\hat{c}_{k}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}})\cdot q^{*}\sigma

and

\displaystyle\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{F})^{2}\cdot\sigma=\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}})^{2}\cdot q^{*}\sigma,

where
k=1,2
.

To see that these classes are well-defined, it is equivalent to show that the intersection numbers are independent of the choice of the modification
q\colon Z\rightarrow X
.
We first observe the following fact.

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
X
be compact complex analytic variety of dimension
n
and let
\rho\colon\widetilde{X}\rightarrow X
be a proper bimeromorphic morphism.
Let
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in H^{2k}(\widetilde{X},\mathbb{R})
.
Assume that there is a closed analytic subset
V\subseteq X
of codimension at least
k+1
, such that

\alpha_{1}|_{\widetilde{X}^{\circ}}=\alpha_{2}|_{\widetilde{X}^{\circ}}
,
where
{\widetilde{X}^{\circ}}=\widetilde{X}\setminus\rho^{-1}(V)
.
Then for any class
\sigma\in H^{2n-2k}(X,\mathbb{R})
, we have

\alpha_{1}\cdot\rho^{*}\sigma=\alpha_{2}\cdot\rho^{*}\sigma.

Proof.

Let
E=\rho^{-1}(V)
.
Then there is some class
\delta\in H^{2k}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{X}\setminus E,\mathbb{R})
such that

\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}

is equal to the image of
\delta
in
H^{2k}(\widetilde{X},\mathbb{R})
.
It follows that

\displaystyle(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2})\cdot\rho^{*}\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta\cdot\rho^{*}\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\rho^{*}\sigma\smallsmile\delta)\smallfrown[\widetilde{X}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{*}\sigma\smallfrown(\delta\smallfrown[\widetilde{X}]).

We note that
\delta\smallfrown[\widetilde{X}]\in H_{2n-2k}(E,\mathbb{R})
.
Then
\rho_{*}(\delta\smallfrown[\widetilde{X}])\in H_{2n-2k}(V,\mathbb{R})
.
By assumption, we have
\operatorname{dim}V\leq n-k-1
.
Hence
\rho_{*}(\delta\smallfrown[\widetilde{X}])=0
.
By the projection formula, we have

\rho^{*}\sigma\smallfrown(\delta\smallfrown[\widetilde{X}])=\sigma\smallfrown\rho_{*}(\delta\smallfrown[\widetilde{X}])=0.

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We are in position to prove the following statement.

Proposition 9.3
.

The intersection numbers in Definition
9.1
are independent of the choice of
Z
.

Proof.

We assume the following setting.
Let
q_{j}\colon Z_{j}\rightarrow X_{j}
be proper bimeromorphic morphisms with
j=1,2
.
There is a closed analytic subset
V\subseteq X
of codimension at least 3, such that
X^{\circ}:=X\setminus V
has quotient singularities.
The restriction of the reflexive coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
X^{\circ}
induces an orbifold vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{orb}}
on the standard orbifold structure of
X^{\circ}
.
Let
Z_{j}^{\circ}=q_{j}^{-1}(X^{\circ})
. Then
q_{j}
is an isomorphism on
Z_{j}^{\circ}
.
There is an orbifold
\mathfrak{Z}_{j}
whose quotient space is equal to
Z_{j}
, such that
\mathfrak{Z}_{j}
is standard over
Z_{j}^{\circ}
.
There is an orbifold vector bundle
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb},j}
on
\mathfrak{Z}_{j}
, whose restriction over
Z_{j}^{\circ}

is isomorphic to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{orb}}
over
X^{\circ}
.
Let
\alpha_{j}\in\{\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb},j}),\hat{c}_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb},j}),\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb},j})^{2}\}
, such that
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
are the same type of characteristic class.

To prove the proposition, it is enough to show that, for any
\sigma\in H^{\bullet}(X,\mathbb{R})
, the following equality holds

\alpha_{1}\cdot q_{1}^{*}\sigma=\alpha_{2}\cdot q_{2}^{*}\sigma.

We note that there is a natural bimeromorphic map
\varphi\colon Z_{1}\dashrightarrow Z_{2}
over
X
.
Let
\widetilde{X}\subseteq Z_{1}\times Z_{2}
be the closure of the graph of
\varphi
,
and let
p_{1}\colon\widetilde{X}\rightarrow Z_{1}
,
p_{2}\colon\widetilde{X}\rightarrow Z_{2}
and
\rho\colon\widetilde{X}\rightarrow X

be the natural morphisms.
Then we have

\alpha_{j}\cdot q_{j}^{*}\sigma=p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\cdot\rho^{*}\sigma

for
j=1,2
.
We notice that
p_{1}
,
p_{2}
and
\rho
are isomorphisms over
X^{\circ}
.
Hence if
\widetilde{X}^{\circ}=\rho^{-1}(X^{\circ})
, then we have

(p_{1}^{*}\alpha_{1})|_{\widetilde{X}^{\circ}}=(p_{2}^{*}\alpha_{2})|_{\widetilde{X}^{\circ}}.

By applying Lemma
9.2
, we obtain that

\alpha_{1}\cdot q_{1}^{*}\sigma=\alpha_{2}\cdot q_{2}^{*}\sigma.

This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Remark 9.4
.

We gather some properties on this notion of Chern classes, which can be derived directly from the definition and Lemma
9.2
.

(1)

If
X
has quotient singularities and if
\mathcal{F}
induces an orbifold vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{orb}}
on the standard orbifold structure over
X
, then the Chern classes of
\mathcal{F}
in Definition
9.1
coincides with the linear forms on
H^{\bullet}(X,\mathbb{R})
induced by the orbifold Chern classes of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{orb}}
.

(2)

Let
X^{\circ}\subseteq X
be an open subset with quotient singularities, such that
\mathcal{F}|_{X^{\circ}}
induces an orbifold vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{orb}}
on the standard orbifold structure on
X^{\circ}
,
and that
X\setminus X^{\circ}
has codimension at least 3.
Let
\Delta\in\{\hat{c}_{1},\hat{c}_{2},\hat{c}_{1}^{2}\}
.
If there is some class
\beta\in H^{\bullet}(X,\mathbb{R})
,
whose restriction on
X^{\circ}
is equal to
\Delta(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{orb}})
,
then
\Delta(\mathcal{F})
is the same as the linear form on
H^{\bullet}(X,\mathbb{R})

defined by the intersection with
\beta
.

(3)

Let
M=H_{1}\cap\cdots\cap H_{n-2}
be the complete intersection surface of basepoint-free Cartier divisors in general position.
Then
M
has quotient singularities,
\mathcal{F}|_{M}
is reflexive and inducing an orbifold vector bundle on the standard orbifold structure of
M
, and we have

\Delta(\mathcal{F})\cdot c_{1}(H_{1})\cdots c_{1}(H_{n-2})=\Delta(\mathcal{F}|_{M}),

where
\Delta
is either
\hat{c}_{2}
or
\hat{c}_{1}^{2}
.

With the first Chern class in Definition
9.1
, we can extend the notion of slope stability as follows.
Let
X
be a compact complex analytic variety of dimension
n
,
which has quotient singularities in codimension 2.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent reflexive sheaf on
X
.
For a cohomology class
\alpha\in H^{2}(X,\mathbb{R})
, we can define the slope

\mu_{\alpha}(\mathcal{F})=\frac{\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{F})\cdot\alpha^{n-1}}{\mathrm{rank}\,F}.

Then
\mathcal{F}
is called
\alpha
-semistable if for any nonzero coherent subsheaf
\mathcal{E}\subseteq\mathcal{F}
, we have
\mu_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E})\leq\mu_{\alpha}(\mathcal{F})
.
It is called
\alpha
-stable if the inequality is strict whenever
\mathcal{E}
has smaller rank.
From the item (2) of Remark
9.4
, we see that this definition coincides with the classic one,
if
X
satisfies that every reflexive coherent sheaf of rank 1 on it
has a positive reflexive power which is locally free.

9.B.
Proof of the Bogomolov-Gieseker inequality

We complete the proof of Theorem
1.3
in this subsection.

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
be an orbifold modification as in Theorem
1.2
.
Then
Y
has quotient singularities.
It follows that
Y
is the quotient space of an orbifold
\mathfrak{Y}=(Y_{i},G_{i})_{i\in I}
,
such that the action of
G_{i}
is free in codimension 1.
Since
f
is projective,
Y
is a compact Kähler variety.
Let
\mathcal{E}=(f^{*}\mathcal{F})/(\mathrm{torsion})
.
Then
\mathcal{E}
induces a reflexive coherent orbifold sheaf
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{orb}}
on
\mathfrak{Y}
,
see for example
[
DO23
, Remark 3.4.(5)]
.
We note that
\mathcal{E}
is stable with respect to
f^{*}\omega
.
By
[
DO23
, Remark 3.4.(5)]
again, we deduce that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{orb}}
is stable with respect to the class of
f^{*}\omega
.

By
[
DO23
, Theorem 3.10]
, there is a functorial projective bimeromorphic morphism
p_{i}\colon Z_{i}\rightarrow Y_{i}
,
such that
Z_{i}
is smooth, that
\mathcal{H}_{i}:=p_{i}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{i}/(\mathrm{torsion})
is locally free, and that the indeterminacy locus of
p_{i}^{-1}
has codimension at least 3.
In particular, there is a canonical action of
G_{i}
on
Z_{i}
such that
p_{i}
is
G_{i}
-equivariant,
and that the
(Z_{i},G_{i})
’s induce a complex orbifold
\mathfrak{Z}
with quotient space
Z
.
The collection
(\mathcal{H}_{i})
defines an orbifold vector bundle
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}}
on
\mathfrak{Z}
.
There is an induced projective bimeromorphic morphism
p\colon Z\rightarrow Y
.
In particular,
Z
is a Kähler variety.
Then there is an orbifold Kähler form
\eta
on
\mathfrak{Z}
, see
[
Wu23
, Lemma 1]
.
Let
q\colon Z\rightarrow X
be the natural morphism.
We note that
q^{*}\omega
can be viewed as a semipositive orbifold
(1,1)
-form on
\mathfrak{Z}
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}}
is stable with respect to
q^{*}\omega
for
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{orb}}
is stable with respect to
f^{*}\omega
.
By the same argument of
[
DO23
, Lemma 3.16]
, we deduce that,
for any
\varepsilon>0
small enough,

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}}
is stable with respect to the following orbifold Kähler form on
\mathfrak{Z}

\alpha_{\varepsilon}:=q^{*}\omega+\varepsilon\eta.

By
[
Fau22
, Theorem 1]
,
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}}
admits an orbifold Hermitian-Einstein metric with respect to
\alpha_{\varepsilon}
.
Then, as in
[
Kob14
, Theorem 4.4.7]
, we have

\Big(2r\hat{c}_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}})-(r-1)\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}})\Big)\cdot[\alpha_{\varepsilon}]^{n-2}\geq 0.

The limit of the LHS above, when
\varepsilon
tends to 0, is equal to

\displaystyle\Big(2r\hat{c}_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}})-(r-1)\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{orb}})\Big)\cdot[q^{*}\omega]^{n-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(2r\hat{c}_{2}(\mathcal{F})-(r-1)\hat{c}_{1}(\mathcal{F})^{2}\Big)\cdot[\omega]^{n-2}

This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎
